## Saturday, 12:47 p.m. - The Very Model of a Modern Magic Metagame

*by **Magic** Maj. Gen. Blake Rasmussen* 

Despite Jund's recent reputation as a format warping monster, Modern has actually been surprisingly diverse as of late. Yes, [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and friends won both Grand Prix Chicagoand Grand Prix Lyon, but the Top 8 and Top 16 decks were incredibly diverse. Both Top 8s had seven distinct archetypes represented with the Top 16 containing a few other goodies for you deck junkies out there.

So let's take a magical trip down Modern metagame lane and rehash just what you might expect to see throughout the hall and in the feature match arena at Grand Prix Toronto this weekend.

### Jund

Might as well get it out of the way. Jund has been on a tear this year, finishing first or second in just about every major Modern tournament, starting with the Player's Championship, where Yuuya Watanabe took down the title with the help of [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) and Co. From there, Jund (and, actually, Watanabe too) took second at Pro Tour *Return to Ravnica*, and a pair of firsts and a second between Chicago and Lyon.

The deck utilizes some of the most efficient spells the game has to offer across a multitude of fronts. [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek) and [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) offer hand disruption; [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) , [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) , [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) and more offer ways to kill things; [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) are two of the best two-mana creatures ever; and [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) is possibly the best one-drop in Modern.

Plus, ya know, [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) .

Hard to hate out and incredibly adaptable, the deck tries to do the best thing at every point in the curve, and does so efficiently. Recent additions like [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20121209000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) have further boosted the deck's power and adaptability. Not only will Jund be heavily played this weekend, but it's an odds-on favorite to put at least one copy in the Top 8.
